I'm probably just not putting enough effort in. Does that make sense? Um, well... my appetite is probably fine, I think. I try to eat when I'm supposed to, so I don't worry anyone. But my sleep... I mean, it's a little off lately, but I know everyone gets stressed and has trouble sleeping sometimes, so I really shouldn't complain.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... I try really hard to keep up with everything, especially at work. I don't want to let anyone down or fall behind on the support tickets. But, um... I guess maybe I have been a little slower lately? 

It's just... sometimes I'll be at my desk, and my hands will just sort of hover over the keyboard for a minute. It takes me a bit longer to answer even the really simple tickets, almost like my brain and my body are running on a delayed connection, if that makes sense? 

I'm sure it's just because I haven't been sleeping great, like I mentioned. I really am trying to push through and stay focused. I hope my boss hasn't noticed... is it normal to feel a little sluggish like that when you're just tired and stressed? I'm sorry, I know I just need to drink more coffee and focus better.

Um, well... I really try to keep up with my hobbies, I do. I know it's important to have a good work-life balance and all that. But, um... I guess lately, things just haven't felt the same. 

I used to really love tinkering with old electronics, fixing them up, you know? And cooking, or just putting on a vinyl record to unwind after a long shift. But most days now, I wake up with this... this flat, heavy sadness. And those things that used to sort of reset me, they just... they don't seem to reach me anymore. 

I still try to do them, though! I don't want to just give up on the things I like. I'm sure I just need to try a little harder to appreciate them, right? It's probably normal to lose interest in things for a bit when you're feeling down, isn't it? I really hope I'm not sounding too negative, I'm trying my best to stay positive about it all.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, oh gosh, no. I mean, I really want to be clear about that—I don't have a plan or anything like that. I would never want to do something that would upset people or make a mess of things. 

It's just... sometimes, when things feel really heavy, I catch myself thinking that maybe it would just be easier if I went to sleep and... and just didn't wake up. Like, then nobody would have to deal with me or worry about me falling behind anymore. 

But I'm not actually going to do anything to hurt myself! I promise. It's just a passing thought when I'm feeling overwhelmed, you know? That's probably a pretty common thing to think when you're stressed out and tired, right? I really hope that doesn't make me sound crazy or anything, I'm just trying to be honest with you.

It's just that the things that usually used to reset me—like putting on a record, or cooking, or tinkering with old electronics—they just don't really reach me anymore. \n\nI feel a little guilty even admitting that, because I know I should just be grateful I have hobbies and just try harder to engage with them. I'm probably just not putting enough effort in. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question okay.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Um, honestly... no, not really. I've tried, I really have. I'll sit down with a project or put on a movie that I know I used to love, but it just feels like I'm going through the motions. I don't get that spark from it anymore. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably frustrating to hear. I feel like I'm being difficult or just not trying hard enough to be positive. I really should be able to find something to enjoy, right? I'm sorry if I'm not giving you very good answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you still feel pleasure when you're around friends or loved ones?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I haven't really been seeing anyone lately. I know I should, and I'm sure if I made the effort to go out, I'd probably enjoy it. I just... I don't want to bring anyone else down, you know? My friends are all so busy and doing so well, and I'd hate to be a burden or ruin their good time with how I've been feeling. \n\nSo I mostly just keep to myself right now. I hope that's not the wrong thing to do. I mean, I'm sure I'll get back to socializing once I can just snap out of this and be better company. Does that... is that an okay answer? I'm sorry, I know I should probably be trying harder to see people.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Um, no, no, I don't... I'll sit down with a project or put on a movie that I know I used to love, but it just feels like I'm going through the motions. I don't get that spark from it anymore. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably frustrating to hear. I feel like I'm being difficult or just not trying hard enough to be positive. I really should be able to find something to enjoy, right? I'm sorry if I'm not giving you very good answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you still feel pleasure when you're around friends or loved ones?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I haven't really been seeing anyone lately. I know I should, and I'm sure if I made the effort to go out, I'd probably enjoy it. I just... I don't want to bring anyone else down, you know? My friends are all so busy and doing so well, and I'd hate to be a burden or ruin their good time with how I've been feeling. \n\nSo I mostly just keep to myself right now. I hope that's not the wrong thing to do. I mean, I'm sure I'll get back to socializing once I can just snap out of this and be better company. Does that... is that an okay answer? I'm sorry, I know I should probably be trying harder to see people.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Um, no, no, I don't... I wouldn't actually do anything to hurt myself. I know that would be terrible for everyone. I don't have a plan or anything like that, I promise. \n\nIt's just... well, sometimes I catch myself thinking that... maybe it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Like, if it just happened on its own, then nobody would have to deal with me or worry about me anymore. It's just a passing thought, though, I try not to dwell on it. I know it's a bad way to think and I should be more appreciative of my life. I'm sorry to even bring it up, I hope that doesn't make you think I'm crazy or anything. Is it... is it normal to feel that way sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still give you a little bit of pleasure?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I guess if I really think about it... no, not really. Even the little things just sort of feel flat right now. I've tried, like, sitting outside with a cup of coffee in the morning because I know that's supposed to be nice and relaxing, but... it's just coffee, you know? \n\nI'm really sorry, I feel like I'm just giving you the same negative answer over and over. I know I should be able to find a silver lining somewhere. I don't want you to think I'm not trying to get better. I really am trying to notice the good things, it's just... a little hard right now. I hope that's okay to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: Um, well... my appetite is probably fine, I think. I try to eat when I'm supposed to, so I don't worry anyone. But my sleep... I mean, it's a little off lately, but I know everyone gets stressed and has trouble sleeping sometimes, so I really shouldn't complain. \n\nIt's just... I'll lie there in bed for hours, just kind of listening for apartment noises, you know? Just waiting. And then when I finally do manage to fall asleep, I always seem to wake up way before dawn. I just wake up already tense and completely exhausted... kind of like I never really powered down, if that makes sense? Like a computer left on standby. \n\nI'm sure I just need to work on my sleep habits, maybe look at screens less before bed. I hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it... is it normal to have sleep issues like that when you're in a slump? I'm sorry, I know I just need to try harder to get on a good schedule.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I haven't really been keeping track, to be honest. My clothes still seem to fit about the same, maybe just a tiny bit looser, but nothing major. \n\nI try to make sure I eat enough so I don't get run down, you know? I guess maybe I have been a little slower lately? \n\nIt's just... sometimes I'll be at my desk, and my hands will just sort of hover over the keyboard for a minute. I'm sorry, I know I just need to drink more coffee and focus better.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling restless and fidgety, or do you feel like you're moving more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: Um, I think we... we might have just talked about this a little bit? But, um, yeah, I definitely don't feel restless or fidgety. It's more the... the slow thing, I guess. \n\nLike I was saying, at work, my hands will just hover over the keyboard sometimes. It takes me longer to answer even the really simple support tickets. It's almost like... like my brain and my body are running on a delayed connection, you know? \n\nI really am trying my best to stay on top of things, though. It's okay that I'm a little slow right now as long as I'm still getting the work done eventually, right? I'm really trying not to let it affect my performance too much.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find no pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess lately, things just haven't felt the same. \n\nI used to really love tinkering with old electronics, fixing them up, you know? I just feel like... I don't know, like I deserve whatever bad thing happens next. Does that make sense? I'm sorry, I'm probably not explaining it very well.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess it's been... maybe about a year and two months? Roughly? I feel a little silly even bringing it up, like I should have just adapted better and not let it affect my work, you know? I don't get that spark from it anymore. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably frustrating to hear. I feel like I'm being difficult or just not trying hard enough to be positive. Um, yes, I'd say it's been nearly every day. Like I was saying, most days I just wake up with this... this flat, heavy sadness. And I really do try to push past it, you know? I try to do the things that used to reset me—like putting on a record, or cooking a nice meal, or tinkering with my electronics. But they just don't reach me anymore. 

But I promise I'm not just letting it ruin every single day! I still get out of bed and do my job and try to be productive. I really don't want you to think I'm just wallowing in it. It's just... it's there almost every day, even when I'm trying my absolute hardest to be positive. 

Does that make sense? I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right so you have all the facts.

I feel like I'm letting everyone down, and sometimes I think I just deserve whatever bad thing happens next because I'm not trying hard enough. \n\nBut I know I shouldn't think like that! I'm really trying to be more proactive and fix my mistakes. It's just hard sometimes when you feel like you're constantly dropping the ball. I really am trying to take responsibility for my part in all this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about how you would harm yourself if you were to act on those feelings of not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: Um, oh gosh, no.
